58:["$","p",null,{"children":["If we later realize that we want to make further changes, we can make them in\nthe working copy and then run ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj squash"}],". 59:["$","p",null,{"children":["Alternatively, we can use ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj edit <commit>"}]," to resume editing a commit in the\nworking copy. 81:["$","p",null,{"children":["In the diff editor, use the arrow keys and spacebar to select all lines but the\nlast. Press 'c' to save the changes and close it. You can now inspect the\nrewritten commit with ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj diff -r @-"}]," again, and you should see your deletion of\nthe last line. Unlike ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj squash -i"}],", which left the content state of the commit\nunchanged, ",["$","code",null,{"children":"jj diffedit"}]," (typically) results in a different state, which means\nthat descendant commits may have conflicts."]}]
